and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

TEAEs, infusion-related TEAEs, and SAEs by body weight (<20 kg, 20 to <50 kg, and ≥50 kg) did
not indicate increasing toxicities with increasing body weight. In addition, no safety concerns
emerged from analyses of TEAEs across gender, race, prior anti-GD-2 therapy, or ADA status
(Section 8.2.7).
Finally, efficacy analyses across subgroups by baseline disease status (primary refractory
disease vs. incomplete response to salvage treatment), disease compartment (bone vs. bone
marrow), age, body weight, prior GD2 therapy, and ADA status have shown consistent efficacy
of naxitamab, which supports the premise that no dose adjustments are required based on
demographics or for special populations (Section 8.1.8).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s position that no dose adjustments are required based
on demographics or for special populations. FDA recommends a dosing cap (150 mg per day
and 450 mg per cycle) for patients with BW >50 kg as described above in Section 6.3.2.2.
In addition, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s position regarding ADA status since
immunogenicity data from studies 12-230, 11-009 and 12-116 conducted by MSKCC are
deemed unreliable as the anti-drug-antibody (ADA) assays used in these trials were not
validated. Study 201, conducted by the Applicant using validated ADA assays, only evaluated
ADA in 23 patients. As such, the FDA will issue a PMC to gather additional ADA and PK data to
adequately assess the incidence of ADA and the impact of ADA status on the PK, safety and
efficacy of naxitamab.
6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is
the appropriate management strategy?
The Applicant’s Position:
No formal drug-drug interaction studies have been performed. This is considered acceptable
because it is unlikely that naxitamab will interact with concomitant drugs in a way that will
affect the systemic exposure of either naxitamab or concomitant drugs for the following
reasons:
•

While nonprotein drugs are metabolized via the cytochrome P-450 system and/or renal
or biliary excretion, an antibody like naxitamab, being a protein, is eliminated via
different mechanisms: cellular uptake followed by proteolytic degradation and via
74

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

•

interaction between antibody and its target.
Metabolic competition between antibodies and other proteins have not been described.
Furthermore, interaction at the binding target (GD2) between nonprotein drugs or other
protein drugs and naxitamab is unlikely.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
The Applicant’s Position:
The efficacy evaluation for the BLA is based on an interim analysis of all efficacy data from the
pivotal Trial 12-230 at the data cutoff date. Trial 201 and the retrospective Study 2PR01 provide
supporting efficacy data.
•
•
•

Subjects could enroll to Trial 12-230 more than once and could have different disease
profiles at different enrollments. Therefore, they are counted by enrollment for the
efficacy analysis, and the term “enrollments” is used in place of “subjects.”
Trial 201 provides interim data for enrollments with at least 1 follow-up for assessment
of efficacy endpoints. Subjects could only enroll once in this trial.
The retrospective Study 2PR01 provides medical record data from patients treated with
naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF under compassionate use in Spain.
Trial 12-230

The Applicant’s Description:
Trial Design
Basic Trial Design
The pivotal Trial 12-230 was an open-label, single-center trial including subjects with either
primary refractory disease or incomplete response to salvage treatment. The trial also included
subjects with other disease status at baseline, but they were not included in the efficacy
analysis.
Trial 12-230 had two phases. Phase 1 was a single-center, single-arm, open-label, 3 + 3 doseescalation design. Phase 2 was a single-center, single-arm, nonrandomized, open-label trial.
Phase 1 assessed escalating doses of intravenous (i.v.) naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) in
combination with subcutaneous (s.c.) GM-CSF (Day –4 through 5) during a 10-day cycle
repeated every 2 to 4 weeks. Subjects with no dose-limiting toxicity (as defined in protocol) and
no progression of disease during the first cycle had the option of receiving an additional 3 cycles
if antidrug antibody (ADA) titer was negative. Subjects who completed 4 cycles of treatment
80
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

without complications or disease progression had the option of continuing treatment for up to
24 months from their first dose of naxitamab.
In Phase 2 all subjects receiving naxitamab at 9.0 mg/kg per cycle. Dosing with naxitamab
occurred on Days 1, 3, and 5 of each cycle in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days –4 through 5).
Cycles were repeated monthly through 5 cycles, then every 1 to 2 months for up to 24 months
from trial enrollment or until a subject had received five cycles after a CR or PR was achieved,
whichever came first. The basic Phase 2 dosing design is shown in Figure 8-1. The timing of
naxitamab and GM-CSF administration was the same in Phase 1.
Figure 8-1

Basic Trial Design

Trial Location
The trial is a single-center trial conducted in New York City, New York, United States.
Choice of Control Group
Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, the trial is single-arm with no comparator.
Diagnostic Criteria
The trial enrolled subjects with neuroblastoma diagnosed by 1) histopathology or 2) bone
marrow metastases or MIBG-avid lesion(s) plus high urine catecholamine levels.
Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
Enrollment was open to subjects older than 1 year of age with a diagnosis of neuroblastoma, as
defined in the section on diagnostic criteria above. Subjects must have had high-risk
neuroblastoma (including MYCN-amplified stage 2/3/4/4s of any age and MYCN-nonamplified
Stage 4 in subjects greater than 18 months or age). Phase 1 subjects must have had refractory
or relapsed neuroblastoma that was resistant to standard therapy (standard therapy included
81
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

intensive induction chemotherapy, followed by a variety of consolidation or salvage therapies,
depending on response). Phase 2 subjects must have had primary or secondary refractory
disease in bone or bone marrow, defined as morphologic evidence of neuroblastoma in bone
marrow and/or abnormal 123I-MIBG uptake in osteomedullary sites, or had to be in second or
greater complete response as defined by INRC with 123I-MIBG findings quantitated by Curie
scores. Prior treatment with other anti-GD2 antibody therapies, including murine 3F8,
naxitamab, ch14.18, and hu14.18 was allowed. No chemotherapy or immunotherapy for a
minimum of 3 weeks prior to start of naxitamab was allowed. Moreover, white blood cell (WBC)
count greater than or equal to 1000/µL, absolute neutrophil count greater than or equal to
500/µL, absolute lymphocyte count greater than or equal to 500/µL, and platelet count greater
than or equal to 25,000/µL were required.
Subjects were excluded for existing major organ dysfunction greater than CTCAE Grade 2, with
the exception of hearing loss and hematologic toxicity (defined as suppression of all subtypes of
WBCs, red blood cells, and platelets); active life-threatening infection; history of allergy to
mouse proteins; anti-human anti-GD2 antibody (ADA) titer greater than 1,300 ELISA U/mL, and
history of allergy to GM-CSF.
Dose Selection
Phase 1 assessed escalating doses of intravenous naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) in
combination with s.c. GM-CSF (Day –4 through 5). Dose levels were 0.3 to 3.2 mg/kg/d (0.9 to
9.6 mg/kg per cycle). Subjects came off trial if they had a dose-limiting toxicity, as defined in the
protocol, during the first cycle.
Although a maximum tolerated dose was not attained in Phase 1, 9.0 mg/kg per cycle was
selected for further development and implemented in Phase 2 of the trial: Intravenous
naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion in combination with s.c. GM-CSF
(Day –4 through 5). The choice of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) as the optimal
dose for the Phase 2 part and for further clinical development of naxitamab was a clinical
judgement based on the evaluation of the clinical pharmacokinetics, efficacy, safety, and
tolerability at that time (i.e., including the investigator’s clinical judgement of each subject case
based on individual subject details).
Trial Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
Trial 12-230 was an open-label, single-arm trial.
Each subject received naxitamab with GM-CSF over a 10-day period in a cycle. A cycle started
with 5 days of GM-CSF administered s.c. at 250 µg/m2/d before the start of naxitamab infusions
82
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

(Day –4 to Day 0) (Figure 8-1). On Days 1 through 5, GM-CSF was administered at a dose of 500
µg/m2/d.
On Days 1, 3, and 5, naxitamab was infused i.v. over approximately 30 minutes.
In Phase 1, cycles were repeated at approximately 2- to 4-week intervals through Cycle 4, and
subjects who completed 4 cycles of treatment without complication or disease progression had
the option of continuing treatment for up to 24 months from their first dose of naxitamab.
According to protocol, Phase 1 dose escalation ranged from 0.9 mg/kg per cycle to 10.8 mg/kg
per cycle; however, dose escalation stopped at 9.6 mg/kg per cycle.
In Phase 2, cycles were repeated monthly though 5 cycles, and Group 1 and Group 3 subjects
had the option of continuing treatment every 1 to 2 months for up to 24 months from trial
enrollment or until they had received 5 cycles after a major response (CR or PR) was achieved,
whichever came first. In Phase 2, the selected total dose per cycle was 9.0 mg/kg (3.0 mg/kg
per infusion). See Section 6.2.2.1 for details.
Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation
For adverse events (AEs) suspected to be related to naxitamab, management guidance and
dose modifications were provided in the protocol. In the dose-escalation phase, Phase 1,
subjects came off trial if they had a dose-limiting toxicity during the first cycle. In Phase 2,
subjects were discontinued from naxitamab if their ADA titers exceeded a cutoff limit (>1300
U/mL), but could resume treatment if their ADA titer dropped below the cutoff limit.
Investigators could discontinue trial drug for AEs associated with trial drug, including
unacceptable toxicity or exacerbation of underlying disease.
Administrative Structure
Trial oversight was managed as follows:
•
•
•

Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center (MSK) served as the sponsor of the trial and
was responsible for clinical trial management and data monitoring.
Y-mAbs contracted clinical research associates who were independent of the trial to
conduct on-site and remote data monitoring for the data used in the BLA.
Y-mAbs commissioned the development of an electronic data capture system, and data
entry support, data management oversight, and statistical analyses were performed by
a contract research organization (CRO).

All laboratory assessments, including PK and ADA assessments, were conducted by MSK
laboratories.
83

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

In addition, independent efficacy assessments were contracted by Y-mAbs: blinded, external,
independent reviews of medical images and of pathology slides were conducted by
board-certified radiologists and board-certified pathologists, respectively.
Procedures and schedule
Bilateral bone marrow aspirates and biopsies and imaging (CT, MRI, MIBG scan, and/or PET) for
response evaluation were performed before treatment, during treatment before Cycle 3, and
after Cycle 5 (Phase 1), and approximately every 3 months during Phase 2 and during follow-up.
Complete history, physical exam, blood pressure, hematology, biochemistry, liver and kidney
laboratory tests, urinalysis (Phase 1 only), pregnancy test, echocardiography (Phase 1 only),
and electrocardiogram (Phase 1 only) were conducted prior to treatment and where relevant
during treatment and follow-up. Blood for ADA assessment was drawn before each treatment
cycle. Adverse events were recorded continuously and up to 30 days after the last treatment or
until the subject began a new treatment, whichever was earlier.
Trial Endpoints
The BLA is submitted for Accelerated Approval based on the surrogate endpoint of overall
response rate (ORR), defined as the sum of partial (PR) and complete responses (CR), supported
by duration of response (DoR), which is defined as the interval between first response and
progressive disease. ORR is recognized as an acceptable endpoint supporting accelerated
approval for single-arm trials, as discussed and agreed with the FDA during a pre-IND meeting.
The response to therapy for neuroblastoma was assessed by the International Neuroblastoma
Response Criteria (INRC), according to the revised INRC updated in 2017.[15]
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
The statistical analyses for the interim analysis were defined in the protocol. A standalone
statistical analysis plan (SAP) was made. There is only one version of the SAP. The SAP was
signed after the statistical analyses of efficacy had started. The principal features in the SAP
followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated from the protocol in the following key
features:
• Additional data (e.g., demographics, baseline characteristics, safety, efficacy subgroups) and
corresponding analyses, to provide and facilitate an overall interpretation of the results,
were specified in the SAP.
• The per protocol set analyses were not included in the SAP.
The analyses performed followed the SAP.

Efficacy Analysis
In Phase 2, subjects were enrolled into one of three subject groups described as follows:
•
•
•

Group 1: Subjects with primary refractory disease (no prior relapse, but incomplete
response to treatment) in bone and/or bone marrow, as documented by histology and
or 123I-MIBG scan.
Group 2: Subjects in second CR or very good partial response and risk for another
relapse. The VGPR criterium was removed from Group 2 enrollment criteria with
Amendment 20.
Group 3: Subjects with secondary refractory disease (prior relapse and incomplete
response to retrieval therapy) in bone and/or bone marrow as documented by histology
and/or 123I-MIBG. (Note: hereafter this will be referred to as incomplete response to
salvage treatment.)

Subjects from Group 2 had no tumor at baseline, therefore, this subject population is not part
of the efficacy population but only contributes to the safety population.
The two efficacy analysis groups are the FAS (full analysis set) and PAS (primary analysis set).
Both groups enrolled subjects with baseline disease defined in Groups 1 and 3 (primary
refractory disease and incomplete response to salvage therapy, had evaluable disease at
baseline, and began at least one infusion of naxitamab). The PAS was restricted to enrollments
treated with greater than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (cycle total of greater than or
equal to 9.0 mg/kg), completing at least one cycle of naxitamab, irrespective of trial phase. The
SAF (safety analysis set) was for the safety analysis and included all subjects that began an
infusion of naxitamab.
Subgroup analyses based on relevant demographic and prognostic factors for efficacy
evaluation were prespecified in the SAP.
Primary efficacy endpoint
The primary efficacy endpoint for the interim analysis of the pivotal Trial 12-230 was the ORR
calculated along with a 95% confidence interval using the Clopper-Pearson exact methodology.
As agreed at the pre-IND meeting with FDA, effectiveness was concluded if the lower limit of
the exact 95% confidence interval was above 20%.
Secondary efficacy endpoint
The duration of response (DoR) was estimated using Kaplan-Meier methods and presented in a
Kaplan-Meier plot. DoR was censored at the date of last disease assessment in subjects who did
85

not experience PD. As a conservative sensitivity analysis, DoR was also analyzed with
descriptive summary statistics in which the subjects were considered to have progressed at last
disease assessment.
Lack of evaluable disease at baseline
In line with International Council for Harmonisation E9 guidance (Section 5.2.1), subjects who
lacked evaluable disease at baseline, as determined by the independent reviewers, were
excluded from the efficacy analysis sets. All subjects in the Trial 12-230 Primary Analysis Set
[PAS] had a completed baseline response assessment.
Safety Analysis
Safety analyses are defined and presented in Section 8.2.
PK Analysis
PK analysis is defined and presented in Section 6.2.1.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the description of the trial provided by the Applicant with the
exception of the definition of the efficacy population. FDA modified the proposed
efficacy population to exclude additional patients based on the dose of naxitamab
received, presence of progressive disease at baseline, radiation exposure to target
lesions, lack of evaluable disease on central review, and timing of baseline disease
assessment relative to completion of prior systemic therapy. Also, although the trial
permitted patients to enroll multiple times in different cohorts, FDA considered only the
first enrollment by each patient. The efficacy population as determined by FDA
consisted of 38 unique patients, compared to 43 unique patients and 45 total
enrollments in the Applicant’s analysis.
FDA deemed it appropriate to revise the Applicant’s definition of the efficacy population
to include patients with baseline evaluable disease who initiated at least one infusion of
naxitamab (as opposed to having completed at least one cycle) and received greater
than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg per infusion. Two patients in the FDA efficacy analysis set
received 3.2 mg/kg per infusion. For patients who enrolled in Study 12-230 more than
once and received naxitamab dosages within this range during each enrollment, data

The Applicant’s Position:
The trial was conducted in accordance with the protocol, applicable regulatory requirements,
International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) Good Clinical Practice (GCP), and with the
Declaration of Helsinki (1989). The investigator conducted all aspects of this trial in accordance
with all national, state, and local laws of the applicable regulatory agencies.
Before protocol-specified procedures were carried out, consenting professionals explained full
details of the protocol and trial procedures as well as the risks involved to participants before
their inclusion in the trial. Participants were also informed that they were free to withdraw
from the trial at any time. All participants signed an IRB/PB-approved consent form indicating
their consent to participate. This consent form met the requirements of the Code of Federal

Protocol Violations/Deviations
The Applicant’s Position:
Most protocol deviations were minor and concerned missed assessments, assessments out of
window, and issues that were included in later protocol amendments (e.g., naxitamab dosing
past that allowed by the protocol at the time, providing informed consent by phone for
potential subjects with travel issues).
Nineteen subjects had at least one protocol deviation that was considered major. The type of
major deviations fell into three types: informed consent, safety, and trial medication
administration. Events reported as major protocol deviations included the following:
• Delays in reporting serious adverse events (SAEs) to the IRB (6 subjects)
• Blood draws for pretreatment assessment before signing consent (1 subject)
• Signature page of the consent having the incorrect amendment number (3 subjects)
• Trial medication administration issues that included the following:
− Period between GM-CSF administration and start of naxitamab infusion (4 subjects)
− Changes in GM-CSF administration days (2 subjects)
− Missed or incorrect dose of GM-CSF (3 subjects)
− Discontinuation of GM-CSF (2 subjects)
− Change in dosing date for naxitamab (1 subject)
− Multiple interruptions to naxitamab administration since the eCRF could only capture
one (1 subject)
Four enrollments having major protocol deviations were part of the PAS group. No subjects
were excluded from the PAS group due to major protocol deviations.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the description of protocol violations/deviations in 12-230 based on the
information provided. The protocol violations/deviations described above are not
expected to have a significant impact on the validity of the safety or efficacy analyses
for Trial 12-30.

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment Compliance
All investigational products were prepared and administered in-clinic. Naxitamab compliance
was calculated as the difference between the calculated planned dose (last recorded predose
body weight x dose level in mg/kg) and the actual dose recorded in the subject CRFs. Calculated
compliance for the first cycle averaged 100.1% (±2.92 with a median of 99.9%). Over 25% of the
infusions were within ±1% of the calculated dose, with 80.8% of the infusions being within ±5%
of the calculated dose and 96.0% of the infusion within ±10%. Individual compliance
calculations ranged from 124.7% to 25.8%.
Concomitant Medications
Concomitant systemic therapy for neuroblastoma was not allowed during the trial.
However, subjects who developed an ADA titer greater than or equal to 1300 U/mL and
therefore, could not have timely treatments with naxitamab and GM-CSF were eligible to
receive low-dose maintenance regimens such as irinotecan alone, temozolomide alone,
irinotecan-temozolomide, or cyclophosphamide-topotecan. They could also be treated with
anti-ADA agents such as rituximab and cyclophosphamide. Naxitamab and GM-CSF treatments
could be resumed when the ADA titer fell below 1300 U/mL.
Pretreatment was allowed before naxitamab infusions with analgesics for pain (e.g.,
hydromorphone) and antihistamines (e.g., diphenhydramine or hydroxyzine).
No major protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited concomitant medications were
reported.
Rescue medication
Surgical and radiotherapy was allowed for subjects whose neuroblastoma progressed. It was at
the discretion of the investigator to decide if naxitamab treatment was to be continued.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Additional information was requested regarding the use of chemotherapy for patients
97
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}

who developed an ADA titer greater than or equal to 1300 U/mL on treatment. Six of 43
patients in the efficacy population had a positive ADA test on treatment and one
received systemic therapy on study. This patient received rituximab (with celecoxib
premedication) and cyclophosphamide and did not demonstrate an objective response.
Therefore, the reviewer is not concerned that receipt of chemotherapy administered to
patients who had a positive ADA contributed to any objective responses observed in this
trial based on the information provided.
FDA notes that radiation was permitted in Trial 12-230 as clinically indicated after cycle
1. Some patients received radiation to sites of disease prior to the onset of an objective
response; patients included in the efficacy analysis who received radiation are listed in
Table 8-7 below. Based on receipt of radiation to some target lesions, the best overall
response for some patients were reassessed as a PR compared to the prior assessment
of CR. Areas of overlap between sites of target lesions and sites of radiation therapy are
highlighted in yellow below. Radiation was administered soon after treatment was
initiated in all cases. As summarized in the table, based on the information available,
this reviewer considers that the observed objective responses cannot be attributed to
administration of radiation in these patients; however, administration of radiation may
have prevented progression in the irradiated lesions, which would have resulted in an
overall response by INRC of disease progression. Concomitant administration of
radiation must be considered when interpreting the efficacy results of the trial.

several review issues in FDA’s evaluation of the primary endpoint, including the method for
determining the overall response by INRC, the method of handling soft tissue disease in the
assessment according to the INRC, and the assessment and potential confounding of the
efficacy results due to a short interval between prior systemic therapy and the baseline scan for
Trial 201.

Review Issue: Assessment of Overall Response per INRC
Individual components of the responses used assess the response to naxitamab using the revised
INRC (measurement of soft tissue lesions, bone marrow evaluations, and MIBG/FDG evaluations)
were independently reviewed and the charters for the independent review of the components
of response was included in the BLA; however, the overall assessment of response based on the
INRC was made by the Applicant based on the independently reviewed components. During the
review, FDA requested detailed listings including detailed information about the inclusion of soft
tissue lesions in the overall assessment of response and evaluated the overall response
determination per INRC made by the Applicant.
Reviewer assessment: The reviewer considered that the overall response per INRC as determined
by the Applicant was acceptable in the context of this trial.
Review Issue: Assessment of Soft Tissue Lesions in Overall Response Assessment
The Applicant provided an overview of response assessment in soft tissue lesions. The Applicant
stated that exclusion of patients with soft tissue disease at baseline was at the investigator’s
discretion and that patients with soft tissue disease expected to be scar tissue were permitted to
enroll. Baseline soft tissue disease was followed by CT/MRI and evaluated independently, but the
external radiology review was not asked to assess whether disease represented active tumor,

local radiation to sites of soft tissue disease is not expected to contribute to the responses
observed given that these sites were not considered target lesions and only progression (and not
responses) in soft tissue would result in a change in overall assessment of response.

Review Issue: Duration between Prior Therapy and Baseline Disease Assessment
FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline
disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the
urgency to enroll patients, particularly with primary refractory disease who may have
demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive
chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy
(i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since patients with
progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated
first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or chemotherapy and immunotherapy) prior to
enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline
disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed
to naxitamab) is greater in this population.
FDA performed a sensitivity analysis excluding patients (non-responders and responders) from
the efficacy population who had a baseline scan < 2.5 weeks from the time of completion of
systemic therapy. In Trial 12-230, this criterion applied to 5 responders and 4 non-responders,
105

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

resulting in 9 responders among 30 patients (ORR 30%), slightly lower than the ORR without the
sensitivity analysis.
Because this sensitivity analysis excluded responding patients with durable complete responses
which the review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy, FDA decided to
only exclude those patients who received systemic therapy up until the time of the baseline scan
(i.e., those patients with a time from systemic therapy to baseline scan of zero weeks) from the

Interpretation of duration of response is limited by the lack of systematic follow-up of
responders after discontinuation of treatment with naxitamab. The protocol did not
require continued assessments of response after end of treatment, patients may have
been followed at another institution, and some received subsequent anti-cancer therapy
prior to demonstration of progression. FDA inquired if a later data cut-off for evaluation
of duration of response was available for a more mature characterization of DOR, but
given the limitations in study assessments and data collection, additional data was not
available. It is therefore uncertain whether these limitations have resulted in an
underestimation of duration of response.

Dose/Dose Response
The Applicant’s Position:
In an analysis of Trial 12-230 by dose level (≥3.0 mg/kg/infusion (45 enrollments (PAS)) vs
<3.0 mg/kg/infusion (15 enrollments)) the ORRs appeared similar between the two groups.
However, importantly the CR rate was higher in the ≥3.0 mg/kg dose group compared to the
< 3.0 mg dose group (31.1% vs 20.0%). Achieving a CR is of major clinical significance. Based on
the information above, the dosing regimen of 9 mg/kg per cycle was determined to present the
best balance between a manageable safety profile while ensuring effect of naxitamab
treatment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA notes that the data presented regarding the efficacy of doses < 3.0 mg/kg/dose is
limited and should be interpreted with caution.
Durability of Response
The Applicant’s Position:
This is addressed under the secondary endpoint, Duration of Response, above.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA has no further comments.
Persistence of Effect
The Applicant’s Position:
This is addressed under the secondary endpoint, Duration of Response, above. No long-term
efficacy data, with the exception of those presented in the preceding sections, are available at
the time of this application.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant that data regarding the persistence of effect beyond
discontinuation of naxitamab is not addressed in the Application.

Trial 201
The Applicant’s Description:
Trial Design
Trial 201 is an ongoing, multicenter, international, single-arm, nonrandomized, open-label,
Phase 2 trial to evaluate the safety and efficacy of naxitamab administered with GM-CSF in
high-risk neuroblastoma subjects with primary refractory disease or incomplete response to
salvage treatment in bone and/or bone marrow. In contrast to Trial 12-230, subjects could only
be enrolled once in Trial 201.
In Trial 201, subjects underwent the same naxitamab treatment cycles as described for Trial
12-230. Treatment cycles were repeated every 4 weeks (±1 week) until CR or PR followed by
five additional cycles every 4 weeks (±1 week). Subsequent cycles could be repeated every
110
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

8 weeks (±2 weeks) through 101 weeks from first infusion at the discretion of the investigator.
After end of treatment (EOT), each subject will enter a long-term follow-up for 3 years in which
they will be monitored at the referral site.
In agreement with the FDA, Trial 201 data were based on an interim analysis and provide
support that the efficacy results obtained in Trial 12-230 are reproducible. However, the
interim analysis was not statistically powered to demonstrate efficacy.
Trial Location
The trial is being carried out at three sites: Two sites in the United States (9 subjects) and one
site in Spain, Europe (16 subjects).
Choice of Control Group
Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, the trial is single-arm with no comparator.
Diagnostic Criteria
The trial enrolled subjects with neuroblastoma diagnosed by 1) histopathology of tumor biopsy
or 2) bone marrow aspirate or biopsy indicative of neuroblastoma by histology plus high urine
catecholamine levels or MYCN amplification or MIBG-avid lesion(s).
Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
Enrollment was open to subjects older than 1 year of age with a diagnosis of neuroblastoma, as
defined in the section on diagnostic criteria above. Subjects should have high-risk
neuroblastoma with either primary refractory disease or incomplete response to salvage
treatment, evaluable in bone and/or bone marrow; if disease was only present in bone, the
subject should have evaluable disease outside the radiation areas for being eligible.
The subject should have acceptable hematological status and acceptable liver and kidney
function as defined in the protocol.
Subjects were excluded for any systemic anticancer therapy, including chemotherapy or
immunotherapy within 3 weeks before the first dose of GM-CSF. Subjects were also excluded
for evaluable neuroblastoma outside bone and bone marrow sites.
High-risk neuroblastoma (NB) was defined as INRG [15, 16] MYCN-amplified any INRG stage of
any age, or MYCN-nonamplified with INRG Stage M subjects, diagnosed at 18 months of age or
older.
111
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Primary refractory disease was defined as no relapse or PD, but incomplete metastatic
response (SD, MR, PR defined by INRC) to treatment in bone and/or BM as documented either
by histologic evidence of NB in bone or BM and/or abnormal 123I-MIBG uptake in skeletal sites.
In addition, at trial enrollment, subjects could not be more than 18 months from initiation of
chemotherapy and must have received at least 4 cycles of standard induction chemotherapy for
high-risk NB.
Incomplete response to salvage treatment was defined as relapse or PD, and incomplete
response (SD, MR, PR, but not PD, defined by INRC) to salvage chemotherapy or MIBG therapy
immediately before enrollment. Furthermore, it was restricted to lesions in bone and/or BM as
documented either by histologic evidence of NB in bone or BM and/or abnormal 123I-MIBG
uptake in skeletal sites. In addition, a minimum of 2 months from last MIBG/PET-CT
documented relapse or PD prior to enrollment was required.
Dose Selection
As in Trial 12-230, the dose level was 9.0 mg/kg per cycle administered on Days 1, 3, and 5 of
each cycle (3.0 mg/kg per infusion) in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days –4 through 5).
Trial Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
Trial 201 is an open-label, single-arm trial.
As in Trial 12-230, each subject received naxitamab with GM-CSF over a 10-day period in a
cycle. A cycle started with 5 days of GM-CSF administered s.c. at 250 µg/m2/d before the start
of naxitamab infusions (Day –4 to Day 0) (Figure 8-1). On Days 1 through 5, GM-CSF was
administered s.c. at a dose of 500 µg/m2/d.
On Days 1, 3, and 5, naxitamab was infused i.v. over approximately 30 minutes.
Dose modification, dose discontinuation
For AEs suspected to be related to naxitamab, management guidance and dose modifications
were provided in the protocol. Investigators could discontinue trial drug for adverse events
considered related to the trial drug.
Administrative structure
Trial oversight was managed by Y-mAbs as the sponsor of the trial. The trial was monitored by a
CRO contracted by Y-mAbs. Furthermore, data management oversight and statistical analyses
were performed by a CRO.
112
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

All laboratory assessments, including PK and ADA assessments were conducted by an external
independent laboratory contracted by Y-mAbs.
In addition, independent efficacy assessments were contracted by Y-mAbs: blinded, external
independent reviews of medical images and of pathology slides were conducted by board
certified radiologists and board certified pathologists, respectively.
Procedures and schedule
Imaging (by CT/MRI and 123I-MIBG scans/FDG-PET or MIBG.SPECT/CT) and bone marrow
pathology (bilateral bone marrow aspirates and bone marrow biopsies) for response evaluation
was performed at screening within 4 weeks before treatment, during treatment before Cycle 3,
and approximately every 3 months. Complete history, physical exam, blood pressure,
hematology, biochemistry, pregnancy test, electrocardiogram, and blood for ADA and PK, were
conducted prior to treatment and where relevant during treatment and follow-up. Adverse
events were recorded continuously and up to 30 days after the last treatment.
Trial Endpoints
The primary efficacy endpoint for the interim analysis was the ORR based on independent,
external, expert assessments, up to the interim data cutoff date. As for Trial 12-230, ORR was
assessed according to the INRC. The secondary endpoint was DoR.
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
A standalone SAP was made for the interim analysis performed to support the BLA. There is
only one version of the SAP. The SAP was signed after the statistical analyses of efficacy had
started. The principal features in the SAP followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated
from the protocol in the following features:
• The definitions of Cohorts 1 and 2 in the SAP were aligned with feedback from FDA and the
subsequently amended protocol.
• Analyses of progression-free survival and overall survival were not included (data not yet
sufficiently mature).
• The ADA-positive subgroup analyses in the clinical trial protocol were omitted: too few
subjects (n=2) were ADA-positive for such analyses to be informative. Instead, subgroup
analyses per relevant baseline disease status and disease compartments were introduced.
The analyses performed followed the SAP.

Efficacy analysis
The interim analyses provide results for the assessment of the interim primary endpoint,
independently assessed ORR, and the secondary endpoint, DoR.
Two cohorts of subjects are enrolled in the trial:
•
•

Cohort 1 includes subjects who are screened negative for ADA and with no preplanned
radiotherapy for metastatic lesions.
Cohort 2 includes subjects who are screened positive for ADA or with preplanned
radiotherapy for metastatic lesions.

Primary and secondary interim efficacy analyses were performed on the full analysis set (FAS).
The FAS consisted of 22 subjects in Cohort 1 who had evaluable disease at baseline, received at
least one infusion of naxitamab and had at least one follow-up before the interim data cutoff
date.
Cohort 1 was the only cohort used for primary and secondary efficacy analysis. Cohort 2
(includes one subject only) was included for exploratory efficacy and safety analyses only.

the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA notes that the study population enrolled in Trial 201 as defined above represents a subset
of patients with relapsed and refractory high-risk neuroblastoma. Patients with progressive
disease at study entry were excluded according to the definition above; this definition has also
been applied in the efficacy population for Trial 12-230. Patients with disease outside the bone
or bone marrow were excluded per eligibility criteria; in Trial 12-230, patients in Phase II were
required to have primary or secondary refractory disease in BM and/or abnormal 123I-MIBG
uptake in osteomedullary sites.
FDA notes that while the description of Cohorts 1 and 2 above states that patients in Cohort 2
could have pre-planned radiation to metastatic sites, patients in Cohort 1 could also receive
radiation to the primary site of disease provided they had not already received radiation to this
site. Patients with primary refractory disease may have discontinued standard first line therapy
for high risk neuroblastoma prior to receipt of radiation to the primary disease site.
Although the protocol excluded patients with evaluable disease outside of the bone or bone
marrow, several patients had soft tissue lesions present at baseline (see discussion in Section
8.1.4).

Unlike the protocol for Trial 12-230, the protocol for Trial 201 provided extensive advice
regarding pre-medication for naxitamab infusion. This was based on the increased frequency

o At the next naxitamab infusion if the subject had a Grade 3
bronchospasm/anaphylaxis at the last infusion

disease, 2 subjects due to an adverse event, 2 subjects completed the trial, and 1 subject met

Protocol Violations/Deviations
The Applicant’s Position:
A total of 48% of the subjects (12 of 25) had major protocol deviations during the period
covered by the trial report. The most common major protocol deviations were pharmacokinetic
sampling performed out of window or missed (three subjects), bone marrow biopsies/aspirates
performed out of window or missed (two subjects), and deviations to eligibility criteria (two
subjects). All other deviations occurred in only one subject each. No deviations affected a
subject’s eligibility for the FAS.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the description of protocol violations and disposition above. Additional
information was requested regarding two eligibility criteria deviations as described below.
Ultimately FDA agrees that eligibility for the FAS was not affected by these protocol deviations.
•

the patient demonstrated metastatic recurrence and was started on treatment as per
COG study ANBL0532 for high risk neuroblastoma and was refractory to this treatment

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the description of baseline characteristics above. Patients had
disease in the bone (59%), bone marrow (9%) or both (32%). All patients had received prior
chemotherapy (100%), 91% had received prior surgery, 36% had received prior radiation, 18%
had received prior anti-GD2 therapy and 18% had received a prior autologous stem cell
transplant. 86% had Stage 4 disease and 18% had MYCN amplification or gain (59% did not have
MYCN gain or amplification and 23% were not reported). Among patients with relapsed disease
(n=8), patients had received a median of 2 lines of prior therapy (range 2 to 3).
FDA notes that patients included in the interim efficacy analysis were enrolled at three sites:
Memorial Sloan Kettering (n=8), Riley Hospital for Children (n=1) and Hospital Sant Joan de Deu
(n=16). See description of post-marketing requirements in Section 13 regarding the need for
additional sites for evaluation of safety and efficacy.
FDA requested additional, detailed information regarding prior therapy and the timing of
receipt of such therapy received by patients included in the efficacy population in order to
better characterize the study population and any potential impact of prior treatment on the
assessment of efficacy of naxitamab. The Applicant provided a detailed listing of therapies
received, the response to such therapies as available, and the timing with respect to enrollment
on Trial 201. FDA’s analysis of the study population indicated that three responders in Trial 201
(b) (6)

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment compliance
All investigational products were prepared and administered in-clinic. Approximately 97% of
123

the actual doses of Naxitamab was equal to the planned dose.
Concomitant medications
Treatment, considered necessary for the subject’s wellbeing (including supportive care) and not
interfering with the assessment of safety and efficacy of the investigational treatment, was
allowed at the discretion of the investigator.
A premedication scheme was recommended to mitigate expected AEs, including pain
associated with infusion of naxitamab.
Pretreatment before naxitamab infusions included analgesics for pain (e.g., hydromorphone),
corticosteroids, antipyretics (acetaminophen), antihistamines (e.g., diphenhydramine or
hydroxyzine), and antiemetics (e.g., ondansetron).
During treatment, the following therapies were not allowed:
•
•
•
•
•

Any additional systemic anticancer therapy
Any local radiotherapy not predefined at baseline
Any live vaccines
Intravenous immunoglobulin
Any investigational medicinal product not defined in the protocol

No major protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited concomitant medications were
reported in the trial.
Rescue Medication
Radiotherapy to primary site
For subjects who were enrolled without having previously received external-beam radiotherapy
to the primary site, this standard consolidative therapy for high-risk NB should be performed
prior to the screening MIBG/PET scans. In circumstances where the subject was in a critical
state with an urgent need of systemic treatment from this trial protocol, the subject could
receive the standard consolidation radiotherapy to the primary site, after completion of at least
one treatment cycle, preferably between Cycle 1 and Cycle 2. The site for external beam
radiotherapy to the primary tumor site was to be predefined and recorded in the CRF at
baseline.
Radiotherapy to metastatic sites
124
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

In circumstances where the subject was in a critical state with an urgent need of systemic
treatment, the subject was allowed to receive external-beam radiotherapy for sites with
bulky/measurable bony metastasis. The radiation should be given after completion of at least
one treatment cycle, preferably between Cycle 1 and Cycle 2. The metastatic lesions for
external beam radiotherapy was to be predefined and recorded in the CRF at baseline. It was a
requirement that the subject had evaluable disease outside the radiation areas to be eligible for
the trial.
No major protocol deviations due to the use of rescue medication were reported.

The FDA’s Assessment:
In Trial 201, the protocol recommended treatment for up to 101 weeks; patients who
demonstrate a response were to receive naxitamab cycles every 4 weeks until 5 cycles after
demonstration of a response, followed by cycles every 8 weeks. Of the 14 responding patients
as of the original data cut-off for the application (June 12, 2019), 7 patients had at least five
cycles of treatment after the onset of response, 3 patients discontinued before reaching 5
cycles after response due to adverse events or progressive disease, and 4 patients had
treatment ongoing. No patients had received 101 weeks of treatment with naxitamab.
FDA performed an evaluation of the receipt of radiotherapy relative to responses observed in
the trial. A total of 6 of 22 (27%) patients received radiation while on treatment with
naxitamab: 4 patients received radiotherapy to the primary site of disease (e.g., abdominal
tumor bed) prior to demonstrating a response to naxitamab (although per the Applicant, the
primary site of disease could not represent a target lesion as soft tissue tumors were not
permitted to be primary lesions), 1 patient received radiation after achieving an objective
(b) (6)
response to naxitamab (patient
, summarized below) and 1 patient did not
demonstrate a response to naxitamab.
•

: Patient initiated naxitamab in
, demonstrated CR in
, and
(b) (6)
received radiation in
. The Applicant reported that this patient did not
demonstrate disease progression prior to receipt of radiation, but received radiation to
the primary tumor because the patient had not yet received radiation typically
administered as part of multiagent first-line therapy.
Reviewer note: The patient appears to have received radiation to a non-target lesion
which would not be expected to impact assessment of response.

There were several review issues in FDA’s evaluation of the primary endpoint, including the
method for determining the overall response by INRC, the method of handling soft tissue disease
in the assessment according to the INRC, and the assessment potential confounding of the
efficacy results due to a short interval between prior systemic therapy and the baseline scan for
Trial 201.
Review Issue: Assessment of Overall Response per INRC
Individual components of the responses used to generate the INRC assessment (measurement of
soft tissue lesions, bone marrow evaluations, and MIBG/FDG evaluations) were independently
reviewed and the charter for the independent review was included in the BLA. The assessment
of overall response per the INRC was made by the Applicant based on the independently
reviewed components. During the review, FDA requested detailed listings including information
about the inclusion of soft tissue lesions in the overall assessment of response and reviewed the
overall response assessments made by the Applicant.
Reviewer assessment: The reviewer considered that the overall response per INRC as determined
by the Applicant was acceptable in the context of this trial.
Review Issue: Assessment of Soft Tissue Lesions
The Applicant provided an overview of response assessment in soft tissue lesions. The Applicant
stated that exclusion of patients with soft tissue disease at baseline was at the investigator’s
discretion, although the eligibility criteria excluded patients with soft tissue disease. The
Applicant stated that patients with soft tissue disease expected to be scar tissue were permitted
to enroll. Baseline soft tissue disease was followed by CT/MRI and evaluated independently, but
the external radiology review was not asked to assess whether disease represented active tumor,
and MIBG/PET results have not been considered in the assessment of soft tissue according to the
review charter.
Reviewer note: The INRC assessment of soft tissue disease incorporates use of MIBG/FDG for
assessment of response and in defining target lesions. FDA requested additional information
including baseline FDG avidity in soft tissue lesions to assess the APPEARS THIS WAY ON
ORIGINAL
Local radiotherapy was permitted to soft tissue disease present at baseline during the trial, and
as soft-tissue responses were not incorporated into the overall response assessment by INRC
since these were not considered target lesions, radiation is not expected to contribute to the
responses observed. The development of new soft tissue lesions or progression in existing soft

Review Issue: Duration from Prior Therapy to Baseline Disease Assessment
FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline
disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the
urgency to enroll patients, particularly those with primary refractory disease who may have
130
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive
chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from completion of
prior therapy (i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since
patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease
were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or chemotherapy and
immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not
reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial
cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population.
In order to address this review issue, FDA performed a sensitivity analysis excluding patients
(non-responders and responders) who had a baseline scan < 2.5 weeks from the time of
completion of systemic therapy from the efficacy population. In Trial 201, this criterion applied
to 2 responders and 4 non-responders, resulting in 8 responders among 16 patients (ORR 50%,
95% CI: 25.5, 74.5), similar to the ORR observed in the overall efficacy population. Because this
sensitivity analysis excluded responding patients with durable complete responses which the
review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy, FDA decided to only
exclude those patients who received systemic therapy up until the time of the baseline scan (i.e.,
those patients with a time from systemic therapy to baseline scan of zero weeks) from the
efficacy population; this did not apply to any patients in Trial 201.

Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
The Applicant’s Position:
Not applicable

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA has no further comments.

Retrospective Study 2PR01
The Applicant’s Description:
Trial Design
Study 2PR01 is a retrospective, noninterventional, noncomparative, single-center,
observational study in patients with high-risk neuroblastoma. The patients were treated in a
compassionate-use program for named patients. The study was a medical records review,
where data were collected from patient medical records and recorded in eCRFs. The objectives
were to evaluate the safety and efficacy of naxitamab + GM-CSF and to get an overview of
patient characteristics and distribution in the categories of patients treated with naxitamab.
The study included 8 categories of patients with neuroblastoma. Categories 1 and 2 (patients
with primary refractory disease in bone and/or bone marrow only and patients with relapsed
disease with incomplete response to salvage treatment in bone and bone marrow only,
respectively) overlap with the subject population in Trials 12-230 and 201. Category 3 (patients
with MRD, defined as patients with CR who were MRD positive by qPCR of bone marrow)
provide supportive information on the efficacy evaluation of naxitamab. Patients from the
remaining categories provide data for the evaluation of the safety of naxitamab.
Trial Location
One site in Spain, Europe.
Choice of Control Group
Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, and due to the retrospective character of the
study, there was no control group.
139
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Diagnostic Criteria
Not applicable due to the retrospective character study design.
Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
To be included in the study, a patient had to meet the following criteria:
•
•

Have a diagnosis of neuroblastoma as defined by international criteria (i.e.,
histopathology or bone marrow metastases plus high urine catecholamine levels).
Have been treated with naxitamab + GM-CSF under compassionate use between
01 June 2017 and 30 November 2018.

Dose Selection
All patients included in the application were treated after the same dosing regimen as in Trials
12-230 and 201: the dose level was 9.0 mg/kg per cycle, administered on Days 1, 3, and 5 of
each cycle (3.0 mg/kg per infusion) in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days –4 through 5).
Study Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
For patient categories 1, 2 and 3, treatment was in accordance with the trials 12-230 and 201,
at the discretion of the investigator.
Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation
For patient Categories 1, 2, and 3, this was in accordance with the Trials 12-230 and 201, at the
discretion of the investigator.
Administrative Structure
This retrospective study was sponsored by Y-mAbs Therapeutics. Y-mAbs used CROs to monitor
study data collected from patient records selected under the protocol criteria, to provide EDC
and data management services, and to conduct the statistical analysis.
Procedures and Schedule
For patient Categories 1, 2, and 3 procedures and schedule were in accordance with the Trials
12-230 and 201, at the discretion of the investigator.

Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Study Endpoints
Patient Categories 1 and 2, pooled: Overall response rate (ORR), defined as the sum of PR and
CR, supported by DoR. Investigator-assessed responses were used for response evaluations.
Patient Category 3: The proportion of patients with MRD at baseline who convert from positive
to negative.
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
A standalone SAP was made for the analyses performed to support the BLA. There is only one
version of the SAP. The SAP was signed before the statistical analyses of efficacy started. The
principal features in the SAP followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated from the
protocol in the following feature:
The duration of response was not specified as an efficacy endpoint in the protocol and was
added as an exploratory endpoint in this SAP.
The analyses performed followed the SAP.
Efficacy analysis
Patients could enroll more than once to compassionate-use treatment and could have different
disease profiles at different enrollments. However, in the patient Categories 1 and 2, there
were 6 unique patients, and in patient Category 3, there were 13 unique patients (i.e., all only
enrolled once).
The full analysis set (FAS) comprised the pooled Categories 1 and 2 encompassing 6 enrollments
(6 unique patients). In addition, for Category 3, efficacy data on MRD was evaluated as
supportive evidence for the effect of naxitamab. The Category 3 FAS comprised 13 enrollments
(13 unique patients).

incomplete data collection that renders interpretation of the results of the study
difficult. Further, the efficacy results were determined by investigator assessment rather
than independent central review, which FDA generally recommends for assessment of
ORR in single arm trials to minimize the potential for bias.

Integrated Review of Effectiveness
The FDA’s Assessment:
The clinical and statistical review teams conclude that taken together, the data from
Study 12-230 and Study 201 provide substantial evidence of the effectiveness of
naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, in pediatric and adult patients with relapsed or
refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have
demonstrated a response of stable disease or better to prior therapy.
Data to Support Contribution of Effect of GM-CSF
The Applicant, at FDA’s request, provided rationale to support the contribution of effect
of sargramostim (GM-CSF) to the proposed regimen. GM-CSF is currently approved for
multiple indications with a recommended dose of 250 mcg/m2/day administered
intravenously or subcutaneously; the dose for the indication “BMT [bone marrow
transplantation] failure or engraftment delay,” suggests a third course of 500
mcg/m2/day can be used for 14 days in the event neutrophil recovery has not occurred
with two courses of 250 mcg/m2/day for 14 days. The Applicant cites literature
supporting the role of GM-CSF in combination with immune therapy to enhance cellmediated cytotoxicity, as well as the approval of dinutuximab in combination with IL-2
and GM-CSF in patients with neuroblastoma.
The Applicant provided results from MSK which tested mu3F8 and GM-CSF in different
combinations in vitro, demonstrating that mu3F8 mediated granulocyte antibodydependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) in a dose-dependent manner with maximal lysis
at 10 μg/mL and half-maximal lysis at 0.1 μg/mL. (Kushner and Cheung, 1989) Adding
GM-CSF translated to a tenfold or greater reduction in the concentration of mu3F8
required for mediating granulocyte ADCC. Table 8-18, which includes data from the
referenced publication, was provided by the Applicant in support of the contribution of
effect of GM-CSF.

Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The Applicant’s Position:
Table 8-13 summarizes the key efficacy results from the three trials supporting the use of
naxitamab for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or
bone marrow in combination with GM-CSF. In the pivotal Trial 12-230, treatment with
naxitamab at greater than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg/d (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) led to a durable,
statistically significant (p=0.0011) and clinically meaningful ORR of 42.2% in the PAS population,
as evaluated by the independent reviewers. The CR rate in the PAS was 31.1%. For enrollments
in Trial 12-230 with bone marrow disease at baseline, the CR rate was 80.0% (95% CI: 56.3%,
94.3%).
The robustness of these results is supported by the consistent benefit of naxitamab treatment
on ORR in Trial 201, in Study 2PR01, and across subgroups by baseline disease status (primary
refractory disease vs. incomplete response to salvage treatment), disease compartment
(presence of bone disease vs. bone marrow disease), age, body weight, prior GD2 therapy, and
ADA status. Although the supporting analyses by trial and subgroups were not powered to
show statistical significance, it is noted that the majority of analyses met the prespecified limit
of 20% for the lower bound of the 95% CI.
Overall, the enrollment demographics and baseline disease characteristics for Trials 12-230 and
201 and retrospective Study 2PR01 (Categories 1 and 2) were similar. The trial populations
analyzed for efficacy were considered to be sufficiently similar to allow comparison of the
efficacy results across the trials. In addition, these demographics are sufficiently representative
of the anticipated patient population that will receive the commercial product in the
United States.
The subjects included in the primary efficacy analyses received naxitamab in combination with
GM-CSF at the planned commercial dose level of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (9.0 mg/kg per cycle)
following the planned commercial regimen of three infusions per cycle on Days 1, 3, and 5 as
150
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

part of a 4- to 8-week cycle. This dose regimen has been shown in these trials to be effective for
the treatment of neuroblastoma in the target patient population.
Taken together, the efficacy data presented in the BLA demonstrate the clinical benefit of
naxitamab for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or
bone marrow in combination with granulocyte-monocyte colony stimulating factor (GM-CSF).
Treatment with naxitamab can address the significant unmet medical need, providing effective
therapy to a patient population currently lacking adequate treatment options.

The FDA’s Assessment:

(Note: Table 13 referenced above in the Applicant’s position has been removed)

The clinical and statistical review teams concluded that the Applicant has provided substantial
evidence of the effectiveness of naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, in pediatric and adult
patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who
have demonstrated a partial response, minor response, or stable disease, to prior therapy.
This application is supported by evidence of a substantial, clinically meaningful and ORR and
durability of responses in this population, which has no approved therapies, in two single-arm
trials. In Trial 201 (n=22), the confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory
high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who demonstrated a response of stable
disease, partial response, or minor response to prior therapy per blinded, independent review
was 45% (95% CI: 24%, 68%), with 30% of the 10 responders having observed duration of
response (DOR) of at least 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 201 included 4
patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment; one (25%) of these patients demonstrated a response.
In Trial 12-230 (n=38), the confirmed ORR per blinded, independent review according to the
revised INRC was 34% (95% CI: 20%, 51%), with 23% of the 13 responders having an observed
DOR of 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 12-230 included 22 patients with prior
anti-GD2 treatment; 4 (18%) of these patients demonstrated a response (95% CI 5%, 40%).
Given the limited number of patients with prior anti-GD2 exposure, the exploratory analyses of
ORR in this subpopulation should be interpreted with caution; however, the results suggest that
naxitamab has clinical activity in patients who received prior anti-GD2 antibody therapy.
FDA considers ORR of a sufficient magnitude with a meaningful duration of response as an
intermediate endpoint likely to predict clinical benefit in this rare population of patients with
relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a
partial response, minor response or stable disease to prior therapy. Importantly, although the
product was studied and is indicated in patients with neuroblastoma in the bone and bone
151
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

marrow, assessment of responses was performed according to the revised INRC, which
incorporates assessment of progression of soft tissue disease and the appearance of any new
soft tissue lesions.
It should be noted that patients in both trials were permitted to receive radiation in certain
circumstances, as detailed in sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.3, although assessment of response
excluded sites that received radiation. Patients who received radiation to the only site of
evaluable disease, which occurred only in some patients in Trial 12-230, were excluded from
the efficacy analysis. Additionally, patients with a best response of progressive disease to prior
therapy (i.e., standard therapy for high-risk neuroblastoma, for those patients with primary
refractory disease or salvage therapy at relapse) were excluded from Trial 201 and from the
efficacy analysis population in Trial 12-230. Therefore, the indication the review team proposes
for approval also excludes these patients.
During the review, the review team considered the impact of a short “wash-out” period
between prior systemic therapy and the timing of baseline disease assessments for both
studies. FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the
baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In
practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly those with primary refractory disease who
may have demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive
chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy
(i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging and potentially
unethical. Since patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with
relapsed disease were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or
chemotherapy and immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of
prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses
observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population.
FDA performed a sensitivity analysis removing patients with a short interval (< 2.5 weeks)
between completion of systemic therapy and baseline disease assessment, which resulted in
similar ORRs in comparison to those noted above in both trials, and also resulted in the
exclusion of patients with durable complete responses that the review team did not consider
reasonably attributable to prior therapy. FDA’s approach was therefore to exclude patients with
no washout period between systemic therapy and baseline disease assessments, which applied
to one patient in Trial 12-230 as described in the review.
The difference in ORR between Trials 12-230 and 201 may be explained by a higher proportion
of patients with relapsed disease, and a higher proportion of patients with prior anti-GD2
therapy, compared to Trial 201; exploratory subgroup analyses in both trials indicated a lower
ORR in patients with refractory disease and in patients with prior anti-GD2 therapy. Though the
152
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

efficacy population across the two studies is relatively small, the review team considers that,
based on sustained responses in heavily pre-treated patients with a high unmet medical need,
the application warrants accelerated approval.
Information on duration of response is limited by several factors. Neither trial systematically
captured imaging assessments after discontinuation of study treatment due to reasons other
than disease progression. In addition, some patients who demonstrated a complete response
were subsequently treated with additional anticancer therapy to consolidate these responses,
which is not an uncommon practice in this disease area given the poor prognosis of subsequent
relapses. Duration of response was therefore censored prior to demonstration of disease
progression in many patients, and the duration of response in the absence of subsequent
therapy is unknown.
Due to small sample sizes and limited follow-up for some patients, there is uncertainty
regarding the magnitude and durability of the treatment effect of naxitamab. The review team
therefore recommends a postmarketing requirement to verify and confirm the clinical benefit
of naxitamab in the indicated patient population by accumulating additional ORR and DOR data
across a sufficient number of clinical sites in ongoing Study 201. Postmarketing requirements
are described in Section 13.

Review of Safety
The Applicant’s Position:
This safety evaluation is based on data from 273 unique subjects spanning 298 enrollments in
four clinical trials and a retrospective study with data collected from subjects treated in a
compassionate-use program. Of the 273 unique subjects, 256 subjects were diagnosed with
neuroblastoma and 17 subjects were diagnosed with other GD-2-positive malignancies.
• Trial 12-230 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 117 subjects, spanning
131 enrollments.
• Trial 201 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 25 subjects.
• Trial 11-009 (naxitamab) includes safety data from 67 subjects (8 of them with other
GD2-positive malignancies).
• Trial 12-116 (naxitamab + interleukin) includes safety data from 12 subjects (9 of them with
other GD2-positive malignancies).
• Retrospective Study 2PR01 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 52 patients,
spanning 63 enrollments.
153
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

All clinical trials are single-arm with no comparator, because of the rarity of the disease and the
high-risk population involved.
The main focus is on safety data for subjects with neuroblastoma exposed to naxitamab in
combination with GM-CSF from Trial 12-230 followed by Trial 201 and 2PR01, then further
supplemented by pooled safety data from Trial 12-230 and Trial 11-009 .
Data from these trials allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of naxitamab in
combination with GM-CSF and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with high-risk
refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow. This safety population is
also considered appropriate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to
provide guidance on toxicity management.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA considered the analysis of safety to be supported primarily by patients in Trial 12­
230 who received the recommended phase 2 dose of naxitamab (n=72) and Trial 201
(n=25). Analyses were also performed for the overall population of patients in Trial 12­
230 (n=117) who received any dose of naxitamab, given that some rare infusion-related
adverse reactions such as anaphylaxis may not be dose-dependent. Safety data from
studies 11-009, 12-116 and 2PR01 was considered supportive and were not included in
the primary safety population for FDA’s review of the BLA.
Safety Review Approach
The Applicant’s Position:
This application is based on a comprehensive analysis of safety data relevant to the use of
naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed
neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow.
Monoclonal antibody cancer therapies in general have a number of recognized immune-related
adverse effects, such as hives, itching, skin rashes, flu-like symptoms, chills, fever, nausea,
vomiting, hypotension, bronchospasm, anaphylaxis, and edema, including angioedema. In
addition, therapies with anti-GD2 mAbs (e.g., the two marketed anti-GD2 mAbs, Unituxin and
Qarziba [dinutuximab beta]), are known to cause pain, hypertension, peripheral neuropathies,
accommodation disorder, and mydriasis.
As expected with this class of drugs, clinical experience confirmed that the expected toxicities
seen with other anti-GD2 mAbs are also the most frequently observed with naxitamab in
combination with GM-CSF.

Review of the Safety Database
Overall Exposure
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety population included all subjects who received naxitamab treatment.
Naxitamab was used in combination with GM-CSF in Trial 12-230, Trial 201, and in 2PR01
patients, as monotherapy in Trial 11-009, and in combination with IL-2 in Trial 12-116.
Safety data comprised 133 subjects with neuroblastoma who received the recommended
naxitamab dose level (3 mg/kg/day for a total dose of 9 mg/kg per cycle) in combination with
GM-CSF: 72 subjects in Trial 12-230, 25 subjects in Trial 201, and 36 patients in 2PR01.
Exposure to naxitamab was considered appropriate to allow for an adequate assessment of
safety in subjects who were representative of the intended target population.
Extent of exposure
Trial 12-230
Trial 12-230 provides the largest source of data for naxitamab exposure. There were
131 enrollments for 117 subjects and all exposed to naxitamab. The mean (SD) number of
naxitamab cycles was 4.8 (3.07), ranging from 1 to 15 cycles. For PAS, the mean (SD) number of
cycles was 5.6 (2.73), ranging from 1 to 13 cycles.
Trial 201
In Trial 201, 25 subjects were exposed to naxitamab. The mean (SD) number of treatment
cycles was 4.6 (2.46), ranging from 1 to 8 cycles.
Trial 11-009
In Trial 11-009, 67 subjects were exposed to naxitamab at doses ranging from 0.06 to
155
