SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
Statistical Issues

The FDA’s Assessment:
There were two major statistical issues in the review of this application.
The first statistical review issue was that in Trial 12-230, each patient may have had multiple
enrollments in the study. To avoid collinearity in the assessment of response in the same
patients over multiple enrollments, only the data from the eligible first enrollment was
considered. This limitation to the sample size in addition to the clinical consideration to
consider only patients with baseline evaluable disease who initiated at least one infusion of 3.0
mg/kg or greater of naxitamab (as opposed to having completed at least one cycle as proposed
by the Applicant) resulted in a final sample size of 38 patients in Trial 12-230.
The second statistical review issue was regarding the follow-up time in Trial 12-230. Given the
structure of the assessments for the study reduced the possibility of longer follow-up for DOR,
the inference that can be made from the study data is limited. In general, FDA is unable to
determine whether the observed results for DOR reflect the actual duration of the treatment
effect or whether the results underestimate the duration of response due to censoring.
Therefore, both the observed proportion of responders with response ongoing at 6 months (26
weeks) was presented in addition to the Kaplan-Meier estimates .

Conclusions and Recommendations

The FDA’s Assessment:

Patients with relapsed and refractory neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have
demonstrated a response of partial response, minor response, or stable disease to prior
therapy represent a population with a serious and life-threatening rare disease for which there
is no FDA-approved therapy and no known curative therapy.
In the opinion of the review team, the submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary
standard for accelerated approval. The recommendation for accelerated approval is based on
the results from two single-arm, open-label trials.
The trials enrolled patients with relapsed or refractory high risk neuroblastoma in the bone or
bone marrow. The primary efficacy population included patients with relapsed or refractory
disease in the bone or bone marrow who had demonstrated a response of partial response,
minor response or stable disease, to prior therapy, had evaluable disease per central review
according to the revised International Neuroblastoma Response Criteria (INRC), and who
235
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

received at least one dose of naxitamab. The primary efficacy population included 22 patients
in Trial 201 and 38 patients in Trial 12-230.
The analysis of efficacy demonstrated a clinically meaningful ORR per independent review with
sufficient duration to indicate a clinical benefit in this relapsed and refractory population. It
should be noted that patients in both trials were permitted to receive radiation in certain
circumstances, though assessment of response excluded sites that received radiation. Patients
for whom radiation was administered to the only site of evaluable disease, which applied only
to patients in Trial 12-230, were excluded from the efficacy analysis. Patients with a best
response of progressive disease to initial therapy or therapy at relapse were excluded from Trial
201 and from the efficacy analysis population in Trial 12-230. Therefore, the indication the
review team proposes for approval also excludes these patients.
The confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma
in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial
response, or minor response to prior therapy in Trial 201 (n=22) per blinded, independent
review was 45% (95% CI: 24%, 68%), with 30% of the 10 responders having observed DOR of 6
months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 201 included 4 patients with prior anti-GD2
treatment. One (25%) of these patients demonstrated a response.
The confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma
in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial
response, or minor response to prior therapy in Trial 12-230 (n=38) per blinded, independent
review according to the revised INRC was 34% (95% CI: 20%, 51%), with 23% of the 13
responders having observed DOR of 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 12-230
included 22 patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment. Four (18%) of these patients demonstrated
a response (95% CI 5%, 40%). Given the limited number of patients, the point estimate for
these results needs to be interpreted with caution; however, the results suggest that naxitamab
has some clinical activity in patients with prior anti-GD2 antibody therapy. The difference in
ORR between the two trials may be explained by the higher percentage of relapsed patients,
who are more heavily pre-treated, in Trial 12-230 (55% vs. 36% in Trial 201).
During the review, the review team considered the impact of a short “wash-out” period
between prior systemic therapy and the timing of baseline disease assessments for both
studies. FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the
baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In
practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly with primary refractory disease who may
have demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive
chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy
236
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

(i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since patients with
progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated
first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or chemotherapy and immunotherapy) prior to
enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline
disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed
to naxitamab) is greater in this population. FDA performed a sensitivity analysis removing
patients with a short interval (< 2.5 weeks) between systemic therapy and baseline disease
assessment, which resulted in similar ORRs in comparison to those noted above in both trials,
and also resulted in the exclusion of durable complete responses that the review team did not
consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy. FDA’s approach was therefore to exclude
patients with no washout period between systemic therapy and baseline disease assessments,
which applied to one patient in Trial 12-230 as described in the review.
Assessment of duration of response was limited by administration of additional anticancer
therapies subsequent to achieving a complete or partial response in patients in both trials.
Neither study was designed to include scheduled assessments of response after completion of
treatment with naxitamab, and Trial 201 is currently ongoing. Additional clinical data from Trial
201 may provide additional data to inform durability of responses.
Although the two trials supporting efficacy and safety in this application used different
manufacturing processes, OBP determined that the products used in each trial were generally
comparable, although direct comparability of potency was not possible given differences in
potency assays used for each product. Refer to OBP assessment memo, p. 32, for details.
Given the rarity of relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma and the lack of an approved
or standard treatment, a randomized trial may not be feasible. The review team considers that
the ORR in the two trials, along with the observed duration of responses, in patients treated
with naxitamab is sufficient to establish clinical benefit in the rare group of patients with high
risk neuroblastoma. Data from additional patients in this patient population including overall
response rate and duration of response, may be required to confirm clinical benefit.
The safety of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF was evaluated in a subpopulation of 97
patients with high-risk neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who had relapsed or refractory
disease, or were in complete remission, from the two open-label, single arm studies used to
support efficacy, Trials 201 and 12-230; patients in the primary safety population received the
recommended phase 2 dose (3 mg/kg/day) of naxitamab intravenously on days 1, 3, and 5 of
each cycle and GM-CSF 250 µg/m2/day subcutaneously on Days -4 to 0 and GM-CSF 500
µg/m2/day subcutaneously on Days 1 to 5. Infusion-related reactions and neurotoxicity were
prominent and potentially severe safety signals evaluated during the review.

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Nearly all patients in both studies (100% of patients in Study 201 and 94% of patients in Study
12-230) experienced an infusion reaction, as defined by a pooled term analysis of events as
described in Section 8.2.5. In Study 201, 68% of patients experienced a Grade 3 infusion
reaction; while in Study 12-230, 32% of patients experienced a Grade 3 infusion reaction and
1.4% experienced a Grade 4 infusion reaction. One patient in Study 12-230 (1.4%) experienced
a cardiac arrest 1.5 hours following completion of an infusion of naxitamab. Given the
prevalence and severity of infusion-related reactions, as well as the need for careful monitoring
and management, IRRs are included as a black box warning in the product label.
Neurotoxicity experienced by patients who received naxitamab included pain, peripheral
neuropathy, prolonged urinary retention, transverse myelitis, neurological disorders of the eye,
and reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome. Pain occurred in 100% of patients in
Study 201 and 94% of patients in Study 12-230. Grade 3 pain occurred in 72% of patients in
Study 201. The USPI advises premedication to treat neuropathic pain. Peripheral neuropathy
including sensory neuropathy, motor neuropathy, paresthesia and neuralgia occurred in 32% of
patients in Study 201 and in 24% of patients in Study 12-230. Neurological disorders of the eye
including mydriasis and blurred vision occurred in 19% of patients in Study 12-230 and 24% of
patients in Study 201, and in some cases were prolonged and had not resolved as of the data
cut-off for the BLA. Prolonged urinary retention beyond discontinuation of opioids, transverse
myelitis, and RPLS were also reported in patients who received naxitamab. Given the severity of
these reactions in some patients, the prevalence of events of neurotoxicity, and the need for
careful monitoring, neurotoxicity was included as a black box warning in the product label.
Hypotension was a prominent component of infusion-related reactions, but hypertension was
also observed in patients who received naxitamab, in some cases associated with RPLS or
causing permanent discontinuation of naxitamab. Hypertension occurred in 44% of patients in
Study 201 and 28% of patients in Study 12-230 who received naxitamab. Grade 3 hypertension
occurred in 4% of patients in Study 201 and 7% of patients in Study 12-230. Hypertension is
included as a warning in the product label.
The most common adverse reactions in Studies 201 and 12-230 (> 25% in either study) were
infusion-related reaction, pain, tachycardia, vomiting, cough, nausea, diarrhea, decreased
appetite, hypertension, fatigue, erythema multiforme,, peripheral neuropathy, urticaria,
pyrexia, headache, injection site reaction, edema, anxiety, localized edema and irritability. The
most common Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (≥5% in either study) were decreased
lymphocytes, decreased neutrophils, decreased hemoglobin, decreased platelet count,
decreased potassium, increased alanine aminotransferase, decreased glucose, decreased
calcium, decreased albumin, decreased sodium and decreased phosphate.

Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

The safety profile of naxitamab demonstrated the potential for severe infusion-related
reactions and neurotoxicity, and the review team felt that a black box warning was indicated to
advise providers of these risks. However, the review team considers that naxitamab may be
safely delivered to patients with appropriate guidance in the product label, oversight from
trained pediatric oncology providers who are experienced in managing similar toxicities, and
administration in infusion centers equipped to respond rapidly to infusion-related reactions.
The clinical reviewers do not recommend a REMS be implemented for naxitamab given these
considerations. Risk management based on labeling, including a black box warning, and routine
pharmacovigilance will be employed to ensure the safe and effective use of naxitamab.
The safety population included patients 1 to 24 years of age across both studies, with a median
age of 5 years in both studies. Although relatively few young adult patients and very young
patients (2 years of age or younger) were enrolled, exploratory analyses of safety trends did not
demonstrate a significant difference in safety across groups.
In summary, the benefit: risk assessment is favorable for the use of naxitamab for the
treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone
marrow who have demonstrated a response of partial response, minor response or stable
disease to prior therapy. Naxitamab has demonstrated significant and clinically meaningful
evidence of anti-tumor activity including confirmed ORR and evidence of durability of responses
in previously treated patients with high risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow. The
safety profile of naxitamab is acceptable given the serious and life-threatening nature of
relapsed and refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow, evidence of
substantial anti-tumor activity, and the lack of approved therapies in this population.
The reviewers recommend accelerated approval under Subpart E (21 CFR 601.41) for
naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, for the treatment of patients with relapsed or
refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a
response of stable disease, partial response or minor response to prior therapy. Accelerated
approval is recommended given the small sample size and need for additional multicenter data
to confirm the efficacy and safety of naxitamab. Clinical benefit will be verified and further
characterized by submission of data on response rate and durability of response from
additional patients who will enroll across multiple sites in the ongoing clinical trial, Study 201.

Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Division did not refer this BLA to the Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC) because,
in the setting of the boxed warning and other advice in the product labeling, the safety profile is
acceptable for the treatment of the indicated population and the application did not raise
significant public health questions regarding the role of naxitamab for this indication. Outside
expertise was not necessary as there were no controversial issues that could benefit from an
ODAC discussion.

10 Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
Neuroblastoma nearly exclusively affects young children: 60% of cases occur before 2 years of
age, and 97% occur by 10 years of age.[2] Naxitamab for the treatment of neuroblastoma was
granted a Rare Pediatric Disease Designation by FDA. In addition, naxitamab for the treatment
of neuroblastoma has orphan drug status; therefore, naxitamab is exempt from the
requirements of PREA.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that naxitamab is exempt from the requirements of PREA. Please see described
demographics for the safety and efficacy populations in Sections 8.1 and 8.2.

13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The following postmarketing requirements and commitments were requested by FDA and
agreed upon by the Applicant. Please also refer to the approval letter for the agreed-upon
timeline for submission of PMR/PMCs and for CMC PMRs.

Nonclinical PMR/PMC
PMR# 3950-3
Rationale for PMR:
There are outstanding questions related to the toxicity of naxitamab, particularly
regarding neuropathic pain and nerve damage associated with the use of the antibody
and reversibility of such outcomes in the indicated patient population. Due to the lack of
changes in nociception in the nonclinical toxicology studies provided in the BLA and the
age of the animals used, an additional toxicology study in juvenile animals of a relevant
species is needed to adequately characterize the risk of the use of naxitamab on the
central and peripheral nerves as related to the indicated pediatric population.
PMR Description:
Conduct a repeat-dose juvenile animal toxicology study (a minimum of 2 cycles) in a
pharmacologically relevant species that is also relevant for the toxicological analysis of
an antibody with immune-mediated effects that will measure the toxicity associated
with the use of naxitamab, particularly effects on the central and peripheral nervous
system. Administration of naxitamab should be reflective of the clinical administration
schedule. Incorporate an evaluation of the effect of treatment on the proximal and
distal nerves, and evaluation of the C1 level of the spinal cord in this study and include
7-8 slices for histopathological assessment of the brain. Evaluate the potential for
effects on nociception and pain threshold, both acute and long-term, both during the
study and at the end of an appropriate recovery period.

252
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Clinical Pharmacology PMR/PMC
PMC# 3950-4
Rationale for PMC:
The BLA for naxitamab included five clinical studies (12-230, 11-009, 12-116, 2PRO1, and 201),
with trials 12-230, 11-009, and 12-116 conducted by MSKCC, with study 12-230 considered to
be the pivotal trial for both the safety and efficacy of Naxitamab. Study 2PRO1 and Trial 201 are
currently being conducted by the Applicant and are evaluating the efficacy and safety of
naxitamab in the indicated patient population. The immunogenicity of naxitamab has not been
adequately characterized as only 25 out of the estimated 85 patients have been enrolled and
had data available for review in the currently ongoing Applicant conducted Trial 201. The
current data from Study 201 provides reliable pharmacokinetics (PK) and anti-product antibody
(ADA) testing results with fully validated PK and ADA assays in a very limited number of patients
(n=4). The PK and ADA results from the three trials conducted by MSKCC are deemed not
reliable because the PK and ADA assays used in these trials are not fully validated. An
immunogenicity testing plan should be incorporated in this trial and any future planned trials to
adequately characterize the incidence of ADA and their impact on PK, efficacy and safety of
naxitamab and provide reliable information to the product labeling.
PMC Description:
Conduct immunogenicity testing in all patients enrolled in the ongoing multicenter clinical trial
(Trial 201) of naxitamab using the validated assays to adequately characterize the incidence of
anti-drug antibody (ADA) and neutralizing antibodies against naxitamab and assess the impact
of ADA on the pharmacokinetics (PK), safety and efficacy of naxitamab in accordance with FDA
Guidance for Industry: Immunogenicity Assessment for Therapeutic Protein Products available
at https://www.fda.gov/downloads/drugs/guidances/ucm338856.pdf. Submit the integrated
immunogenicity assessment report in the final report submission. The results from this study
may inform product labeling.
Clinical PMRs
PMR #3950-1
Rationale for PMR:
Naxitamab is being considered under accelerated approval for the treatment of patients
with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who have
253
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

demonstrated a response of stable disease or better following initial or subsequent
therapy. Approval is based upon the results of a single center study and an interim
analysis for an ongoing multicenter study. Continued approval may be contingent upon
verification and description of clinical benefit through submission of additional efficacy
data from additional patients in the ongoing multicenter clinical trial (Study 201).
PMR Description:
Submit the final report, including datasets, from an ongoing multicenter clinical trial to
verify and further characterize the clinical benefit of naxitamab for the treatment of
patients with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in combination with granulocyte­
macrophage colony-stimulating factor (GM-CSF) in bone or bone marrow who have
demonstrated a partial response, minor response, or stable disease to prior therapy;
and to provide a more precise estimation of overall response rate and duration of
response (according to the revised International Neuroblastoma Response Criteria by
blinded independent review). Enroll a minimum of 80 patients with evaluable disease
and systematically follow all responding patients for at least 12 months from the onset
of response, or until disease progression, whichever comes first. Include subgroup
analyses of overall response rate and duration of response by prior anti-GD2 exposure,
disease status (refractory vs. relapsed disease), anti-drug antibody (ADA) status
(neutralizing antibody [nAb] positive versus nAb negative, ADA positive versus ADA
negative), and area of disease involvement (bone marrow vs. bone) in the final report
reflecting a sufficient number of patients in relevant subgroups. Derive the clinical data
from a minimum of six study sites, with at least five patients enrolled per site in at least
five sites.
PMR# 3950-2
Rationale for PMR:
Serious infusion reactions have occurred with naxitamab, necessitating a boxed
warning. The product labeling included the following terms, occurring the day of or the
day following infusion, to capture serious infusion reactions: hypotension,
bronchospasm, flushing, wheezing, stridor, urticaria, dyspnea, pyrexia, infusion related

reaction, face edema, swelling face,

(b) (4)
swelling, tongue edema, swollen tongue, mouth swelling,

(b) (4)
edema, chills, hypoxia, pruritis, erythema multiforme, and

majority of the safety data is derived from a single center, where the product was
developed and management of these serious reactions at this center may not be
representative of experience at additional centers. Post-marketing data will not be

sufficient to characterize the risk given the level of detail available regarding

interruptions and discontinuation of naxitamab. Due to the limited, single-arm nature of
the safety database of naxitamab at the time of this accelerated approval, additional
clinical data from the ongoing multicenter study is needed to adequately characterize
the risk in a broader, multicenter experience.
PMR Description:
Conduct a comprehensive analysis evaluating and characterizing the incidence, clinical
presentation, management, and outcome of the potential serious risk of naxitamab­
associated infusion-related reactions in a sufficient number of patients to adequately
characterize the risk. Submit an integrated final report containing data using the
preferred terms included in product labelling from patient-level and pooled analyses of
on-going and completed clinical trials, post-marketing reports and/or literature reports
and a comprehensive pharmacovigilance assessment for this potential serious risk. The
report should also include an analysis of pre-medication administered. The results from
this report may inform product labelling.

OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)
19.4.1 Population PK Analysis
The Applicant conducted a population PK analysis to characterize the PK of naxitamab, identify
covariate factors that could affect naxitamab disposition and compare the individual exposure
estimates for subsequent exposure-response analyses. Data were collected across the trials 11­
009, 12-116 and 12-230 conducted at the Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center (MSKCC) and
Trial 201 conducted by the Applicant. The studies included in the population PK analysis and the
PK sampling times are shown in Table 19-3.

270
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Table 19-3. Overview of studies included in the population PK analysis.

Source: The Applicant’s population PK report (hu3F8-MS-02), Page 23, Table 1.
The majority of patients in the primary patient population were white (74.3%) and 14.2% were
Asian. The median age of all patients was 7 years old (range: 1 - 34), 65.5% were males and the
median body weight (BW), body surface area (BSA), lean body mass (LBM) and body mass index
(BMI) were 22.2 kg (range: 11.5 - 90 kg), 0.87 m2 (range: 0.5 – 2.12), 15.8 kg/m2 (range: 12.4 –
30.8) and 15.8 kg/m2 (range: 12.4 – 30.8), respectively. The majority (n=82, 72.6%) of the 113
patients were of normal liver function, 30 patients (26.5%) with mild hepatic impairment, and 1
(0.9%) with moderate hepatic impairment based on NCI hepatic impairment classification. The
majority of the 113 patients had normal (n=99, 87.6%) or mildly impaired (n=13, 11.5%) renal
function, and 1 (0.9%) had moderate renal impairment. No patients with severe renal

{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

impairment or severe hepatic impairment were enrolled in these clinical trials. Demographic
data and laboratory values for patients in each of the three trials are shown in Table 19-4.
Table 19-4. Summary of Demographics

272
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4707829

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761171}
{DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

Source: The Applicant’s population PK report (hu3F8-MS-02), Page 27, Table 2.
The population PK analysis was conducted via nonlinear mixed-effects modeling with the
NONMEM software, version 7.4 using first-order conditional estimation with INTERACTION
option (FOCE+I). Concentrations below the limit of quantification (BLQ) were excluded from the
PK analysis. The PK samples obtained in MSKCC trials 11-009, 12-116 and 12-230 were analyzed
with a partially-validated assay and the LLOQ was not defined. For Trial 201, a validated assay
with a LLOQ of 70 ng/mL was used. This LLOQ of 70 ng/mL was also considered for the MSKCC
trials. In total, 50 observations were reported as concentration below BLQ, which were
excluded from the analysis.
A total of 1743 PK observations from 113 patients were included in the population PK analysis.
The plasma concentrations of naxitamab were described by a four-compartment disposition
model with linear elimination. The model included inter-individual variability (IIV) for CL and V1.
The residual error model included proportional error terms. Covariates included sex, race, age,
BW, BMI, BSA, LBM, ALT status, AP status, eGFR status, IL-2 coadministration and study effect.
The final population PK parameter estimates and bootstrap estimates for naxitamab are
presented in Table 19-5. The final PK model was parameterized in terms of CL, V1, Q1, V2, Q2, V3,
Q3, and V4. Estimated fixed and random effect parameters were estimated with good precision
(%RSE < 16%). The magnitude of the interindividual variability was moderate for CL (30.2 %CV)
but high for V1 (100.8 %CV). Residual variability was moderate. The PK of naxitamab is affected
by BW, IL-2 coadministration and study effect. Study effect on CL is likely a combination of
different infusion times, lack of actual dosing and sampling time points in the MSKCC trials, lack
of PK samples obtained during the terminal phase in the MSKCC trials, and the use of different
bioanalytical assays in the MSKCC trials and Trial 201. BW was included in the final population
PK model following allometric principles, where volume and clearance parameters increase less
proportional to body weight (exponents are 0.577 and 0.323, respectively).

The diagnostic plots for the final PK model are shown in Figure 19-1. The VPC (visual predictive
check) on the final PK model in Figure 19-2 is stratified by trial. The model described the
observed data well and the model predictions were generally within the 90% prediction
intervals. No apparent bias was observed in the overall model fit for the data. Bootstrap results
are in good concordance with the final model parameter estimates. (Table 19-4)

and Cmax are expected to increase with increasing BW as a result of the proportional increase in
total administered dose and less than proportional increase in naxitamab CL (Figure 19-3, Table
19-6, Figure 19-4 and Table 19-7).

In total, ADA formation was observed in 40 patients (25.5%) at any time during treatment with
naxitamab; however, only a small subgroup (n=18) of patients, providing both (positive) ADA
data and naxitamab PK data at the day of ADA formation or thereafter during Cycle 1, was
available for the evaluation of the impact of ADA formation on the naxitamab PK. The ADA
effect was not evaluated in the population PK analysis.
In Trial 201, positive ADA titers were observed in 2 patients (8.7% out of 23 patients), with a
total of 4 PK samples obtained at the same day or after positive ADA titers were observed.
Figure 19-5 showed the individual naxitamab PK profiles. In general, reduced naxitamab
concentrations during the first treatment cycle were observed in patients with ADA formation
for which PK data were available at the time of ADA formation. Because PK data are not
available for subsequent treatment cycles in these patients, it is uncertain whether the
naxitamab exposure changes were due to ADA formation.

Reviewer’s comments:
The population PK model developed by the applicant was verified by the reviewer. The model
appears to be acceptable in general because of the good agreement between observations and
predictions. Based on the population PK model, naxitamab exposures are expected to increase
with increasing BW (Figure 19-4). The mean predicted AUC0-672h is approximately doubled in
patients with BW 70-80 kg compared to the patients with BW 20-30 kg.(Table 19-6). Flat dosing
in adults and older adolescents with higher BW will minimize PK variability. There were limited
clinical safety experiences in patients with BW > 50 kg with doses > 450 mg per cycle (i.e., 3
patients with BW >50 kg received dose >450 mg/cycle with 2 patients each only received 3
infusions and 1 patient received 5 infusions) who are expected to have higher exposure
compared to that observed in the clinical trials. A dosing cap of 450 mg per cycle of naxitamab
for patients with BW > 50 kg was suggested by the reviewer. With the dosing cap, predicted
naxitamab exposures for patients with BW higher than 50 kg are comparable to patients with
lower BW (30 - 50 kg) at weight-based dosing of 3 mg/kg/day (9 mg/kg per cycle). (Figure 19-6)

19.4.2 Exposure-Response for Efficacy Analysis
The exposure-response relationships of overall response rate (ORR) or complete response (CR)
and naxitamab exposure were evaluated with 22 patients in trials 201 and 17 patients in Trial
12-230. Neither naxitamab exposure (AUC), nor any of the evaluated covariates were found to
have a significant effect on ORR or CR. Average daily naxitamab exposure in Cycle 1 was
comparable between responders and non-responders. Patients without prior anti-GD2
treatment had a slightly higher ORR or CR rate compared to those with prior anti-GD2
treatment, and ORR/CR rates were higher in Trial 201 compared to Trial 12-230. (Table 19-9)

Reviewer’s comments:
The results of the exposure-efficacy analysis for naxitamab were verified by the reviewer.
Logistic regression analyses identified similar shallow and non-significant relationships between
exposures (AUC24h, Cmax, Ctrough) of naxitamab and ORR. Further analysis for CR resulted in similar
findings (Figure 19-7). Overall, the ER relationships between ORR/CR and exposures of
naxitamab are inconclusive because of the limited data from the trials in small number of
patients and potential confounding factors (e.g., ADA effect).

19.4.3 Exposure-Response for Safety Analysis
The relationships between adverse event (AE) responses (Grade 2+ bronchospasm,
hypertension and hypotension) and naxitamab exposures (AUC24h) at steady state, age, sex,
body weight, race and baseline blood pressure were evaluated in patients with advanced
tumors in trials 11-009, 12-116, 12-230 and 201. The occurrence of Grade 2+ bronchospasm,
hypertension and hypotension events were summarized by covariates of interest. (Table 19-10)

(Grade 2+ bronchospasm, hypertension and hypotension) rates were observed in Trial 201
compared with other trials (Table 19-9). As shown in Table 19-9, patients in MSK trials (Trial 11009, 12-116, 12-230) had lower occurrence rates of Grade 2+ bronchospasm than patients in the
Y-mAbs-sponsored trial (Trial 201). Figure 19-10 and Figure 19-11 indicate that the patients with
prior anti-GD2 treatment might have lower Grade 2+ bronchospasm rate than patients without
prior anti-GD2 treatment and a possible relationship between naxitamab AUC24h and occurrence
of bronchospasm events, but both were not identified in the analysis.

treatment (Figure 19-13). Table 19-9 and Figure 19-14 show that patients in MSK trials (Trial 11009, 12-116, 12-230) had lower occurrence rates of Grade 3 hypotension than patients in the Ymabs trial (Trial 201). Figure 19-15 illustrates that there is a possible relationship between
naxitamab AUC24h and occurrence of grade 3 hypotension, but it was not identified in the
analysis.

11-009, 12-116 and 12-230
